to be e v_i(c_i+1) if v_i(a)<=0 and the unique crossing occurs at step
c_i>=a; put it0 if no crossing occurs or if v_i(a)>0. Define

    I_i^leak=sum_(t=a)^(N-1)(h_t/2) Q_(i+1)(t) 1_(v_i(t)>0),
    M_after(a)=sum_(t=a)^(N-1) h_t,
    R_i^hor=W_i(a)-L_i M_after(a)/2+C_i^cross+e beta I_i^leak.

Summing ONLY steps satisfying the proved horizontal guards gives

    W_i(N)<=R_i^hor.                                          (8)

This applies on the actual suffix. The no-entry branch has the full
initialized W_i(0) derived by (4), including -beta D and -D.
The last-entry branch has the EXACT entry from the preceding actual
query s=a-1, h_s=b_j:

    W_i(a)=r_i(s)+alpha(Q_(i-1)(s)-beta Q_i(s))
              +e[v_i(s)+alpha(P_(i+1)(s)-Q_i(s))]_+.           (9)

Here entry implies Q_(i-1)(s)>0, x_i(s)<ell_i, r_i(s)<0 and U_i(s)=0.
Neither the negative initialized r_i nor either neighboring derivative
is discarded. The later mass includes the full following H_j.

At a terminal horizontal high state r_i(N)>L_i, beta e<1 eventually and
z_i(N)=beta r_i(N)+v_i(N) imply (z_i(N))_+<=W_i(N)/e.
For v_i>=0 this uses beta r_i+v_i<=(r_i+e v_i)/e; for v_i<0
it uses z_i<=beta r_i<=r_i/e. Equations(5),(8) therefore give

    Phi_i(x_n)<=(L_i+sigma_i/e)(R_i^hor)_+                    (10)

on this terminal branch. The terminal edge is paid by the SAME mixed
residual, not an independent cap substitution.

### A2. Remove the immediate incoming source without losing its debt

For2<=i<=k-1 introduce the SIGNED incoming inventory

    rho_i=r_i+z_(i-1)/kappa.

Direct substitution of the FULL laws (1) at i and i-1 gives

    rho_i^+=rho_i+alpha beta/kappa
                          [Q_(i-2)-U_(i-1)-beta P_i].         (11)

The large Q_(i-1) cancels, with no independence or cap assumption.
At the actual own checkpoint,

    z_(i-1)(t_i)=beta r_(i-1)(t_i)-ell_(i+1)/beta
                                                    +beta D_(i+1),
    rho_i(t_i)=beta r_(i-1)(t_i)/kappa
              -ell_(i+1)/(beta kappa)-beta^3 D_(i+1)/kappa.   (12)

r_(i-1)(t_i) is its ACTUAL full-prefix value, not a previous checkpoint
margin. Formula(12) displays the negative initialized terms explicitly.

At the actual entry (9), (11) gives the alternative exact form

    W_i(a)=rho_i(s)-z_(i-1)(a)/kappa
       +alpha beta/kappa[Q_(i-2)(s)-U_(i-1)(s)-beta^2 Q_i(s)]
       +e[v_i(s)+alpha(P_(i+1)(s)-Q_i(s))]_+.                (13)

This pays the direct incoming source as a change in its edge inventory.
It creates a specific SIGNED debt: -z_(i-1)(a)/kappa can be positive
after a large overshoot. A bound on the positive incoming edge alone
does not pay this debt. Replacing that signed inventory by its positive
part would discard the actual source payment and reintroduce the old
independent-cap loss.

The two direct attempts thus sharpen the interface rather than close it:
(8) has only a single downstream crossing and beta-weighted edge leakage;
(13) cancels Q_(i-1) exactly but needs its actual post-entry negative edge
overshoot, the older Q_(i-2), and the initialized (12). No trajectory
estimate pays these together. No actual initialized counterexample was
found. The old universal cross-time and margin-only assertions are not
retested and are not used.

## 3. Comparison B: actual terminal edges and chronological horizontal exits

### B1. A finite initialized edge bound on its exact horizontal guard

Define for a middle i

    Z_i=(beta C+3/4+4C^2)sigma_i,
    A_i^edge=beta(sigma_(i-1)+sigma_(i+1))+L_(i+1),
    gamma_i=3kappa sigma_i/4-beta(sigma_(i-1)+sigma_(i+1)).

A_i^edge is an edge SOURCE allowance, distinct from the static threshold A_i.
Suppose r_i<=L_i and z_i>Z_i at an ACTUAL query. Let
g=(P_i,-Q_i) be its projection and compare with the feasible endpoint
b=(beta sigma_i,-sigma_i). The projection normal inequality is

    (v_i-Q_i)(sigma_i-Q_i)<=(r_i-P_i)(P_i-beta sigma_i).

If r_i<=0, (2) already implies Q_i=sigma_i. If r_i>0,
0<=P_i<=L_i, and the right side is at most L_i^2 whenever it is positive.
Indeed if P_i>=beta sigma_i and r_i>=P_i, bound each factor by L_i;
if P_i>=beta sigma_i and r_i<P_i the product is nonpositive; if
P_i<beta sigma_i and r_i<P_i it is at most P_i beta sigma_i<=L_i^2;
the remaining case again has nonpositive product.

If Q_i<=3sigma_i/4, dividing by sigma_i-Q_i>=sigma_i/4 gives
v_i<=3sigma_i/4+4C^2 sigma_i. Then
z_i=beta r_i+v_i<=Z_i, a contradiction. Consequently

    r_i<=L_i and z_i>Z_i => Q_i>3sigma_i/4.                   (14)

Q_i>0 implies x_(i+1)<ell_(i+1), hence U_(i+1)=0. Equation(1)
therefore proves on this actual high-edge guard

    z_i^+<=z_i-alpha gamma_i.                                (15)

No h(2-h) term or distance descent is invoked. At any query the full
positive source in its z update is at most A_i^edge, so an entry step
can add at most alpha A_i^edge; all negative terms remain valid losses.

Assume now the explicitly REACHABLE-STATE HYPOTHESIS

    r_i(t)<=L_i at EVERY actual final-suffix query0<=t<=N.     (16)

It is a condition on the prescribed trajectory, not a replacement state.
If z_i(N)>Z_i and there is a LAST entry into z_i>Z_i at h=b_j,
all later actual samples satisfy the guards of(15). The actual following
word contains H_j, giving

    z_i(N)<=Z_i+[b_j A_i^edge-gamma_i S_j]/2.

The exact original mass relation is
b_j=d S_j+c-1-d(1-v_mass)2^j with v_mass=a_word/d in(0,1).
Thus d A_i^edge<=gamma_i yields

    z_i(N)<=Z_i+(c-1)A_i^edge/2.                              (17)

This retains every legal bridge and pays the complete following subtree.

The above-threshold-since-start branch MUST use its initialized history.
Using(4), the own-checkpoint negative charge, the entire prefix up to
t_k, and the full positive sources in(1), gives

    z_i(0)<=-kappa D_(i+1)+A_i^edge S_pre/2.                  (18)

Here S_pre is a legitimate upper bound on the whole prefix mass; it is
not a simultaneous checkpoint snapshot. If A_i^edge S_pre<=gamma_i S,
(15),(18) imply z_i(N)<=-kappa D_(i+1)<0, contradicting that branch.
This pays its actual incoming data, including the old neighbor.

Here are concrete uniform FINITE sufficient conditions, not a numerical
claim that they hold at any specific untested depth:

    beta<=1/128, C<=65/64, 2<c<=5/2, S_pre/S<=3/2,
    sigma_(i-1)/sigma_i<=5/2, sigma_(i+1)/sigma_i<=7/16.        (19)

They hold eventually by the jointly derived scale limits, since the
limiting ratios are lambda and lambda-2 and S_pre/S->sqrt(2).
C<=1+2beta follows from sqrt(1+2beta)<=1+beta. Under(19),

    A_i^edge/sigma_i<=957/2048,
    gamma_i/sigma_i>=1489/2048,
    d A_i^edge/sigma_i<=957/4096<1489/2048,
    A_i^edge S_pre/(sigma_i S)<=2871/4096<1489/2048.

Also Z_i<5sigma_i and (c-1)A_i^edge/2<sigma_i/2.
Hence the FULL actual initialized edge, under(16),(19), obeys

    (z_i(N))_+<11sigma_i/2,
    Phi_i(x_n)<=L_i^2+11sigma_i^2/2<7sigma_i^2.               (20)

This is a conditional result within the joint unverified argument.
It is not an assertion that(16) holds from e_1.
If(16) held for every middle i, (6),(20) would give
(B_n/R_m^2)V_mid,m->0 and the frozen function's total scaled payoff
would tend to T_infty<5/64. This says nothing about unrestricted W_n.

### B2. Retain the actual datum at the last horizontal exit

If r_i(N)<=L_i but(16) is not known, let a_i^exit=0 when(16) holds.
Otherwise let a_i^exit=1+max{t<N:r_i(t)>L_i}. This is the actual
query immediately after its LAST horizontal high sample. Every query
from a_i^exit through N satisfies r_i<=L_i.

Apply the edge-entry argument on exactly this remaining interval.
Either a later LAST edge entry pays its source by(17), or all remaining
edge samples are high and(15) gives z_i(N)<=z_i(a_i^exit)
-gamma_i M_after(a_i^exit)/2. Define the ACTUAL exit debt

    E_i^exit=[z_i(a_i^exit)-gamma_i M_after(a_i^exit)/2
                              -Z_i-(c-1)A_i^edge/2]_+.

Then

    (z_i(N))_+<=Z_i+(c-1)A_i^edge/2+E_i^exit,
    Phi_i(x_n)<=7sigma_i^2+sigma_i E_i^exit.                  (21)

For a_i^exit=0, the initialized calculation(18),(19) pays this debt
and E_i^exit=0. For a genuine exit it is a new, explicit chronological
datum, with both neighbors present in the exact update

    z_i(a_i^exit)=z_i(s)-alpha[kappa Q_i-beta Q_(i-1)
                          -beta Q_(i+1)+beta U_i-U_(i+1)](s),
    s=a_i^exit-1.                                           (22)

An exit may not inherit the initialized bound(18). No entrance to a
zero cone, horizontal guard or bounded exit edge is silently inferred.

### B3. An exact scoped failure of a source-free horizontal-exit reset

The claim that every horizontal exit automatically has z_i=O(sigma_i)
is false for the SAME frozen function, even with no incoming gradient
and no downstream horizontal gradient. The following is an ALTERNATIVE
state test, not an initialized trajectory or a lower witness.

Choose2<=i<=k-2 and a physical available h=b_(m-2), alpha=h/2, with
alpha>1 and alpha beta>C/2. Choose q in(0,1) close to1 and write p=p(q).
Set

    t=[(alpha-1)sigma_i p+L_i/2]/(p-beta)>0,
    r_i= sigma_i p+t(p-beta)=alpha sigma_i p+L_i/2,
    v_i= sigma_i q+t(q+beta),
    x_i=A_i+r_i, x_(i+1)=ell_(i+1)-v_i,
    x_(i+2)=ell_(i+2).

The vector (r_i,-v_i)-(sigma_i p,-sigma_i q)
is t(p-beta,-q-beta), an outward circle normal. Thus its ACTUAL
projection is(P_i,-Q_i)=(sigma_i p,-sigma_i q).

All earlier components can be made zero at both samples by the explicit
recursive choice x_j=min(A_j,ell_j+x_(j+1)/beta)-sigma_j for j=i-1,...,1,
using the smaller of the initial and proposed next boundary coordinate
at j=i-1; also enforce x_1<=A_1-beta D_2. Earlier coordinates then have
zero gradients and do not move. All later coordinates after i+2 are0,
except the isolated scalar x_(k+1)=ell_(k+1)+D_(k+1).
The next component has input below ell_(i+1) and output ell_(i+2),
so P_(i+1)=Q_(i+1)=0; all other retired components also vanish.

The actual step therefore has

    r_i^+=L_i/2,
    v_i^+=v_i-alpha sigma_i q
        =(alpha-1)sigma_i beta(p+q)/(p-beta)
                                  +(L_i/2)(q+beta)/(p-beta)>0,
    z_i^+=beta L_i/2+v_i^+.                                 (23)

As q approaches1, p-beta approaches0 while beta>0. For any proposed
constant cap-scale reset, (23) exceeds it. The new input is below L_i,
while the new actual edge residual is arbitrarily large. The next
component remains zero since its input is still below its translation;
all earlier zero-cone choices remain valid since x_i^+>A_i>=ell_i.
The isolated tail excess stays positive for this legal suffix step.

Thus terminal/exit input information alone does not supply a bounded
edge state. The tested normal parameter and huge initial downstream
deficit are not asserted reachable from e_1. The actual initialized
exit debt(21),(22) remains OPEN, not refuted. This is a new exit-reset
failure, distinct from the previously screened cross-time and
simultaneous-checkpoint-margin claims.

## 4. Comparison C: pay the known active-successor source exactly

### C1. A chronological interval with an explicit negative mixed margin

For i<k let a_i^succ=t_(i+1), the actual NEXT selected checkpoint.
Throughout the actual ordinary block and that checkpoint, the active
successor has x_(i+1)>=ell_(i+1) at every pre-step query. Hence
Q_i=0 throughout [t_i,t_(i+1)); this does not reset component i.
All earlier components keep their full update and actual incoming flux.

At the beginning x_(i+1)=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1). Just before the next
selected step it is ell_(i+1)+ell_(i+2)/beta+beta sigma_(i+1),
with P_(i+1)=beta sigma_(i+1). Just after that step it is
ell_(i+1)+ell_(i+2)/beta-beta D_(i+2).
Its WHOLE exact physical horizontal integral, including that selected
step, is therefore

    I_(i+1)^P(i,t_(i+1))
                 =D_(i+1)-ell_(i+2)/beta+beta D_(i+2).        (24)

No source amount is approximated by an independent cap. Define the
remaining ACTUAL incoming/own-work balance over this same interval

    J_i^in=sum_(u=t_i)^(t_(i+1)-1)(h_u/2)
                                      [Q_(i-1)(u)-P_i(u)].

Then(3),(4),(24) give the exact chronological state

    r_i(t_(i+1))=-beta D_(i+1)+J_i^in,
    v_i(t_(i+1))=beta D_(i+2)-ell_(i+2)/beta.                 (25)

J_i^in retains the actual Q_(i-1) and all P_i work. During this
interval Q_i=0, so P_i=clip(r_i,0,L_i). This is an actual driven
horizontal Huber interval, with a fixed fully ordered word; it is not
an executed scalar recurrence or a source-free model.

The static negative margin can be quantified. For i+1>=2 the block
mass is s=S_(l_i), and the exact word mass identity gives

    T_(i+1)=c d s/2+(c-1)^2/2-1
                              -(c d/2)(1-v_mass)2^(l_i).

Since c d-[(c-1)^2/2-1]=(c-1)^2/2>=0 and v_mass<1,
T_(i+1)<=c d(1+s/2). The full-circle denominator satisfies
M_(i+1)>=C(1+s/2), jointly derived in Appendix A. Thus

    D_(i+2)/D_(i+1)<=c d/C.                                 (26)

Under the finite scale condition c d<=1+beta/(2e),
e D_(i+2)/D_(i+1)<=1-beta/(2C). This condition holds eventually
because c d-1=O(1/m) and beta is of order m^(-1/2).
Also beta D_(i+2)>ell_(i+2)/beta eventually, by the joint translation
and denominator bounds. Thus the actual v_i in(25) is positive, and

    W_i(t_(i+1))=J_i^in-beta D_(i+1)
                           +e beta D_(i+2)-e ell_(i+2)/beta
       <=J_i^in-beta^2 D_(i+1)/(2C)-e ell_(i+2)/beta.         (27)

For i=k-1 this is the ACTUAL W_(k-1)(0) at the final-suffix start.
For every other i, all intervening future steps through t_k must still
be retained; its own active-successor state is not substituted at t_k.
Equation(27) identifies a negative initialized mixed margin and the
specific remaining driven flux J_i^in. No bound on that actual flux
at the required scale is proved.

### C2. Pay the source inside the signed terminal-gradient identity

Fix the ACTUAL terminal local projection p_N=P_i(n), q_N=Q_i(n).
The full own-checkpoint identity from the maximizing formula is

    Phi_i(x_n)=-beta D_(i+1)p_N-D_(i+1)q_N
         -(p_N^2+q_N^2)/2
         -sum_(u=t_i)^(n-1)(h_u/2)
              [p_N(P_i-Q_(i-1))-q_N(P_(i+1)-Q_i)](u).       (28)

This is algebra for the ACTUAL terminal support, not a generic
cross-time sign claim. Its full Q_k before the final root is retained.
Split exactly at t_(i+1). By Q_i=0 and(24), the large initialized
-D_(i+1)q_N is paid by the completely known successor P integral.
The resulting equality is

    Phi_i(x_n)=-beta D_(i+1)p_N
              +[beta D_(i+2)-ell_(i+2)/beta]q_N
              +p_N J_i^in-(p_N^2+q_N^2)/2
         -sum_(u=t_(i+1))^(n-1)(h_u/2)
              [p_N(P_i-Q_(i-1))-q_N(P_(i+1)-Q_i)](u).       (29)

All old/future gradients and all later dissipation remain. The
remaining word starts H_(l_i+1),b_(l_i+2),H_(l_i+2),...,
b_(m-1),H_(m-1); for i=k-1 it is exactly the final H_(m-1).
The decomposition is staggered by each actual own checkpoint, not a
simultaneous array of checkpoint margins.

First direct attempt: use the explicit negative mixed margin(27) to
dominate the terminal support in(29). This requires control of the
actual J_i^in AND the later signed integral. A small bound on v_i at
t_(i+1) alone is insufficient: r_i contains the full J_i^in.
The terminal-gradient coefficients p_N,q_N depend on the true reached
state and cannot be freely optimized independently of those histories.

Second direct attempt: telescope the static successor terms across
middle components. If p_i=P_i(n), q_i=Q_i(n), the two neighboring
static D terms in the sum are beta D_(i+2)[q_i-p_(i+1)].
This is -beta D_(i+2) times the ACTUAL combined terminal derivative
at i+1. That derivative is not nonnegative: on a feasible edge-only
terminal support q_i>0 forces U_(i+1)=0 and may have p_(i+1)=0.
Thus the static sum is not a nonpositive boundary term. All staggered
J_i^in terms and all later signed pairings must remain to pay it.
No already-refuted universal cross-time positivity is proposed.

A precise coefficient screen illustrates this failure without making
an initialized counterexample. A feasible pure-edge terminal projection
for component i has p_N=beta q_N, q_N=sigma_i; other retired projections
can be zero. To specify all coordinates, take x_i=A_i,
x_(i+1)=ell_(i+1)-kappa sigma_i, x_(i+2)=ell_(i+2),
then set x_j=min(A_j,ell_j+x_(j+1)/beta)-sigma_j backwards for
j=i-1,...,1, enforcing also x_1<=A_1-beta D_2. Set all remaining
retired coordinates after i+2 to0 and the isolated scalar to
ell_(k+1)+D_(k+1). The zero-cone inequalities show every other retired
gradient vanishes. This defines a feasible single-sample support, not
a chronological history from e_1. Its static coefficient in(29) is

    sigma_i[beta D_(i+2)-beta^2 D_(i+1)-ell_(i+2)/beta]>0     (30)

eventually by the joint amplitude/translation limits. Its negative
gradient norm is only of order sigma_i^2. This refutes the shortcut
that the paid static coefficients alone are nonpositive for every
feasible terminal support. The associated J_i^in and later integral
are NOT claimed zero or reachable with that support. They may cancel
(30), and that actual initialized comparison remains unproved.

## 5. Unified finite actual-terminal comparison and exact unpaid data

Let H_mid={i:2<=i<=k-1,r_i(N)>L_i} and L_mid the other middle indices.
For i in H_mid use the actual horizontal initialization/last entry and
R_i^hor from(8),(9). For i in L_mid use the actual last horizontal exit
and E_i^exit from(21),(22). Under the explicit finite conditions above,
(10),(21) give the full finite comparison

    V_mid,m<=7 sum_(i in L_mid)sigma_i^2
       +sum_(i in L_mid)sigma_i E_i^exit
       +sum_(i in H_mid)(L_i+sigma_i/e)(R_i^hor)_+.            (31)

Define A_m^actual as the sum of the last two charges in(31).
The COMPLETE joint endpoint and tail derivation in Appendix A gives

    B_n*2F(x_n)/R_m^2
       <=T_m+epsilon_end,m
                      +(B_n/R_m^2)[14sigma_1^2+A_m^actual],  (32)

where epsilon_end,m->0 and T_m->T_infty<5/64. This is a comparison
for this ONE frozen function with its TRUE closest radius. It is not
an upper certificate for W_n. Its exact actual charges are:

- Initialized/last-entry W_i, with the negative own-checkpoint terms
  and both neighbors in(4),(9); the strong persistence retains only
  actual beta-weighted outgoing Q flux plus one actual crossing.
- Signed incoming inventory rho_i and the ACTUAL post-entry negative
  z_(i-1) debt in(12),(13), with its older neighboring source.
- The actual terminal edge at the LAST horizontal exit, with full
  subsequent word and remaining loss in(21),(22).
- The known active-successor payment(24) and remaining actual J_i^in
  and terminal-gradient pairing in(27),(29).

If every actual middle input satisfied the full-suffix guard(16),
A_m^actual would vanish and the middle terminal contribution would
vanish at the benchmark scale. That guard is NOT established.
More generally, (B_n/R_m^2)A_m^actual->0 would suffice. A uniform
limsup strictly below1-T_infty would suffice for a scoped strict
terminal deficit. Neither assertion is proved or refuted here.

The alternative-state exit test(23) closes only a source-free reset;
it does not close the initialized entry/exit route. The static-support
test(30) closes only a static sign shortcut, not a chronological
terminal-charge bound. No numerical displayed zero, large proxy X,
cap-only lambda^(2k) allowance or state from another construction is
an actual terminal witness.

## 1. Exact static function, body and physical word

For each integer m>=16 put

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), k=floor(sqrt(m)), J=m-k, beta=1/k,
    eta_word=1-1/(4m), c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)), a_word=eta_word/c,
    d=c-2,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), b_j=1+a_word*c^j,
    S_j=sum H_j, n=2^m-1.

Every child uses this SAME enclosing m and these same parameters. Select
the first occurrences of levels J,...,m-1. Checkpoint i has level
l_i=J+i-1, physical step index 2^l_i-1 and value b_l_i.
The ordinary block masses are s_1=S_J, s_i=S_(J+i-2) for 2<=i<=k,
and the last mass is S=S_(m-1). Every entry of the word is executed.

For 0<=q<=1 define

    p(q)=beta+sqrt((1+beta)^2-(q+beta)^2),
    C=beta+sqrt(1+2beta), e=C-beta=sqrt(1+2beta),
    K=conv({0} union {(p(q),-q):0<=q<=1}),
    kappa=1+beta^2.

The complete horizontal sections of this full body are

    0<=q<=1, beta*q<=p<=p(q).                              (1)

It has the two radial segments to (C,0) and (beta,-1), and the whole
circular arc between them. No finite-plane replacement is made.

For a compact convex body B containing0 put

    E_B(w)=max_(g in B){<g,w>-||g||^2/2}.

Write Hub_L for the one-sided Huber: zero for r<=0, r^2/2 for
0<=r<=L, and L*r-L^2/2 for r>=L.

The local data of every ordinary block are defined using its actual order.
If its physical entries are a_1,...,a_N, set alpha_j=a_j/2.
Start (u_N,-y_N)=(beta,-1), q_N=1, and define backwards

    (q_(j-1)+beta)/(p(q_(j-1))-beta)
       =max{beta/e, (y_j+(1-alpha_j)*beta)/
                              (u_j-(1-alpha_j)*beta)},
    (u_(j-1),-y_(j-1))=(u_j,-y_j)+alpha_j*(p(q_(j-1)),-q_(j-1)).
                                                               (2)

The left side increases continuously from beta/e to infinity. Its right
denominator is positive since u_j>=beta and alpha_j>0. The starting
coordinates for block i are denoted u_i,y_i; set M_i=u_i+y_i/beta.
These are finite symbolic definitions, not a recurrence executed this round.

Fix ell_1=0,D_1=1 and define, before the run,

    T_i=b_l_i/2-1, sigma_i=D_i/M_i,
    ell_(i+1)=sigma_i*y_i, A_i=ell_i+ell_(i+1)/beta,
    D_(i+1)=sigma_i*T_i,
    Phi_i(X,Y)=E_(sigma_i K)(X-A_i,Y-ell_(i+1)),
    delta=D_(k+1)/(1+S),
    F(x)=1/2 sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1))
                      +1/2 Hub_delta(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1)),
    x_0=e_1 in R^(k+1).                                    (3)

We use the range in which every T_i>0; this holds for all sufficiently
large m. This is exactly the frozen circular half-sum.

## 2. Geometry, true radius, and every neighboring gradient

Completing the square in E_B identifies its unique maximizer as Pi_B(w),
the Euclidean projection. The maximizing inequalities at w and w' imply

    ||Pi_B(w)-Pi_B(w')||^2
                <=<Pi_B(w)-Pi_B(w'),w-w'>.

The maximizing formula and this inequality give differentiability with
gradient Pi_B and a 1-Lipschitz gradient. Thus E_B is convex, nonnegative
and1-smooth. Its zero set is the polar cone of B: if some support pairing
is positive, taking a sufficiently small positive multiple of that
support vector gives a positive envelope value.

The cone of K has extreme rays (C,0),(beta,-1). Consequently

    Phi_i=0 iff x_i<=A_i and
                      x_(i+1)>=beta*(x_i-ell_i).            (4)

The disk with center(beta,beta), radius1+beta supports the whole arc.
Its normal and the horizontal endpoint normal prove, in (2),

    Pi_K(u_j,-y_j)=(p(q_j),-q_j),
    u_i=beta+sum alpha_j*p(q_(j-1)),
    y_i=1+sum alpha_j*q_(j-1), u_i>=beta*y_i.                (5)

For the uncapped points the normal ratio is (y_j+beta)/(u_j-beta).
The defining predecessor ratio reduces its numerator by alpha_j*beta
and increases its denominator by the same amount, so q_(j-1)<=q_j.
At the capped endpoint the inequality y<=beta*(u-C)/e is preserved
by every further backward capped step. These facts check the normals
also at the transition and endpoints.

At any global query, define ALL gradients by

    (P_i,-Q_i)=Pi_(sigma_i K)(x_i-A_i,x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1)), i<=k,
    P_(k+1)=clip(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1),0,delta), Q_0=0.

Every physical step is exactly

    x_j^+=x_j-(h/2)(P_j-Q_(j-1)), 1<=j<=k+1.               (6)

In particular earlier components are never reset or deleted.

If x_(i+1)>=ell_(i+1), replacing (P,-Q) by (P,0) improves its
maximization by Q*(x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1))+Q^2/2 when Q>0.
Thus Q_i=0 in that region. If x_i<=ell_i and its output is
nonnegative, (4) gives Phi_i=0.

These properties prove the active-pair chronology inductively. At block i
the active local states are (A_i+sigma_i*u_j,ell_(i+1)-sigma_i*y_j);
its input is >=ell_i, so Q_(i-1)=0 at the ACTIVE pair's queries.
Its next component vanishes, as do all still-future components.
Earlier components retain (6). The selected step produces, exactly,

    x_i(t_i)=A_i-beta*D_(i+1),
    x_(i+1)(t_i)=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1),
    t_i=2^l_i.                                             (7)

Equation(7) specifies only that pair at its OWN checkpoint time.
It is not a simultaneous snapshot of the later retired chain.

The map stacking all adjacent pairs and the last scalar counts each
coordinate at most twice. Its squared norm is <=2. Thus the factor1/2
makes F convex1-smooth. F>=0 and F(0)=0. Its entire minimum set is

    C_min={x: x_i<=A_i,
                x_(i+1)>=beta*(x_i-ell_i), 1<=i<=k;
                                      x_(k+1)<=ell_(k+1)}.

The point (A_1,ell_2,0,...,0) belongs to it. From (5),

    1-A_1=sigma_1*u_1, ell_2=beta*A_1=sigma_1*y_1,
    A_1<=1/(1+beta^2).

For x_1<0 the squared distance from e_1 is at least1.
For 0<=x_1<=A_1 it is at least (1-x_1)^2+beta^2*x_1^2,
decreasing on this interval and attained by that feasible point. Therefore

    R_m^2=dist(e_1,C_min)^2
          =(1-A_1)^2+ell_2^2=(u_1^2+y_1^2)/M_1^2>0.        (8)

All physical samples, terminal samples and minimizers obey every ordered
smooth-convex interpolation inequality. To check this directly, apply
the 1-smooth descent inequality to F(z)-<grad F(y),z>, minimized at y;
it gives F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>>=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.
The Gram matrices are actual Euclidean Gram matrices. Scaling F by an
advertised L>0 and using steps h/L preserves (6) and the normalized payoff.

## 3. Uniform scales and the strictly scoped accepted input

Here are the bounds needed for the normalizations, derived jointly so no
earlier unverified estimate is used as a premise.

In a backward block set w_j=(y_j+beta)/(q_j+beta). Expansion of (2),
using the circular normal at uncapped points, gives

    w_(j-1)/w_j
       =1+[p(q_(j-1))*q_j-q_(j-1)*p(q_j)]/
                         [beta*(p(q_(j-1))+q_(j-1))].       (9)

At a transition to q_(j-1)=0 this follows from y_(j-1)=y_j;
if both q's are zero it is equality. The numerator is the integral
of p(q_(j-1))-q_(j-1)*p'(t). After division by p(q_(j-1))+q_(j-1)
the integrand is <=max(1,-p'(t)). Since w_N=1, multiplication gives

    y_i<=Y_k:=2 exp(2k),
    integral_0^1 max(1,-p'(t))dt=sqrt(2)*(1+beta)-beta<2.

For the integral split at t=(1+beta)/sqrt(2)-beta.
At uncapped points u<=beta+2k*(Y_k+beta). The uncapped backward steps
are contiguous and have p>=beta; their combined deficit from C times
half their mass is <=4k^2*(Y_k+beta). The remaining p's equal C.
Paying also y_i/beta and beta gives

    |M_i-C*s_i/2|<=K_k:=20k^2 exp(2k).                     (10)

The concave function p(q)+q/beta has smaller endpoint C. Using (5),

    M_i>=beta+1/beta+C*s_i/2>=C*(1+s_i/2).                 (11)

Let v_mass=a_word/d. Since c*(c-2)>1>eta_word, 0<v_mass<1.
Substitution in the exact mass relation gives

    1+S_j=v_mass*c^j+(1-v_mass)*2^j,
    b_j=d*S_j+c-1-d*(1-v_mass)*2^j.                       (12)

Uniformly for J<=j<=m-1 the relative leading-mass error is
O(c^(-J)+(2/c)^J). Also k*K_k/S_J->0: its numerator has logarithm
O(k+log k) and the denominator has logarithm J*log(lambda)+O(1).
Using 0<=x-log(1+x)<=x^2/2 with x=1/(4m) yields

    S_J/lambda^J->exp(1/4),
    S_(m-r)/lambda^(m-r)->exp(1/4) for every fixed r>=1.

The first amplitude ratio T_1/M_1 is (d/C)*(1+o(1)); the other amplitude
ratios are (c*d/C)*(1+o(1)). The individual relative errors are uniformly
O(K_k/S_J+c^(-J)+(2/c)^J), and k times this bound tends to zero.
Taking logarithms in the product therefore proves

    D_(k+1)=(d/C)*(c*d/C)^(k-1)*(1+o(1))
                 ->(lambda-2)*exp(-2),
    R_m^2->1, sigma_1~2/(C*S_J),
    sigma_(i+1)/sigma_i=T_i/M_(i+1)->lambda-2               (13)

uniformly for i<k. Here c*d=1+O(1/m) and log(C)/beta->2,
so C^k->exp(2). Eventually every adjacent sigma ratio is in [1/3,1/2],
and sum sigma_i^2<=2sigma_1^2.

The ORIGINAL ALL-SPLIT benchmark is C_0^bench=B_0=1,

    C_n^bench=max_(0<=r<n)Omega(C_r^bench,C_(n-r-1)^bench),
    B_n=max_(0<=r<n)G(C_r^bench,B_(n-r-1)),
    Omega(a,b)=(a+b+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2,
    G(a,b)=2a+b/2+sqrt(b^2+8ab)/2, U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

The circle constant C in this report is distinct from C_n^bench.
Only the unconditional ray conclusion of accepted fact04219554e41d9750
is used:

    0<Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m) B_(2^m-1)<infinity,
    Z_1<5.                                                (14)

The latter follows from its b=1,j=0 upper enclosure, B_1=4, and
(2+3/100)^2<5. No seed hypothesis is assumed.

Put q_root=t_k=2^(m-1), N=2^(m-1)-1. The final suffix is the actual
ordered H_(m-1), indexed t=0,...,N. During this suffix the last excess
is D_(k+1)-delta times half the mass already read, and stays >=delta.
Output isolation gives Q_k=0, proving this claim by induction.
Thus its terminal Huber numerator is exactly D_(k+1)^2/[2(1+S)].

The retired function and its exact updates are

    G_ret(x)=1/2 sum_(i<k)Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1))
                         +1/2 Hub_(C*sigma_k)(x_k-A_k),
    d_i=P_i-Q_(i-1), x^+=x-(h/2)d, Q_0=Q_k=0,
    V_m=2G_ret(x(N))=sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x(N)).

Phi_k denotes the original last circular value, exactly equal to this
horizontal Huber at these actual states. Hence

    B_n*2F(x_n)/R_m^2=T_m+B_n*V_m/R_m^2,
    T_m=B_n*D_(k+1)^2/[2(1+S)*R_m^2]
                  ->T_infty=Z_1*exp(-17/4)/(2lambda)<5/64. (15)

The useful finite-error normalizing scale is

    (B_n/R_m^2)*sigma_1^2*(1+K_k+2^m)->0.                  (16)

Indeed B_n*sigma_1^2=O(lambda^(-m+2k)); multiplying by K_k
still decays exponentially in m. The remaining term is
O((2/lambda)^m*lambda^(2k)), also tending to zero. No numerical
evaluation, finite benchmark replay or m>=1536 inference is used.

## 4. Both endpoint estimates, included in this new joint attempt

For i<k define

    r_i=x_i-A_i, z_i=beta*(x_i-ell_i)-x_(i+1),
    U_i=P_i-beta*Q_i>=0; U_k=P_k, r_k=x_k-A_k.

Changing cone coordinates in (1) gives the exact formula

    Phi_i=max {U*r_i+Q*z_i-[(U+beta*Q)^2+Q^2]/2:
         0<=Q<=sigma_i,
         0<=U<=sigma_i*p(Q/sigma_i)-beta*Q}.               (17)

For r_i<=0 it reduces to

    U_i=0, Q_i=clip(z_i/kappa,0,sigma_i),
    Phi_i=Hub_(kappa*sigma_i)(z_i)/kappa.                  (18)

There are the state-dependent implications

    U_i>0 => x_i>A_i>=ell_i => Q_(i-1)=0,
    Q_i>0 => x_(i+1)<ell_(i+1) => U_(i+1)=0.              (19)

The full coupled residual law is

    r_i^+=r_i-(h/2)(U_i+beta*Q_i-Q_(i-1)),
    z_i^+=z_i-(h/2)(kappa*Q_i-beta*Q_(i-1)-beta*Q_(i+1)
                                         +beta*U_i-U_(i+1)). (20)

At the first checkpoint r_1=-beta*D_2. Since its coordinate subsequently
cannot increase under (6), this inequality persists and U_1=0.
If z_1>kappa*sigma_1, (18) gives Q_1=sigma_1 and

    x_2=beta*x_1-z_1<ell_2-beta^2*D_2-kappa*sigma_1.

Then U_2=0 and P_2<=beta*sigma_2. Write
gamma_1=kappa*sigma_1-beta*sigma_2. In this region z_1 loses
at least h*gamma_1/2; an entry step can add at most h*C*sigma_2/2.

Every occurrence of b_j in the suffix is immediately followed by a
complete H_j. This follows inductively from its physical ruler definition.
Take the LAST entry into z_1>kappa*sigma_1 if the terminal residual
exceeds that threshold. The following subtree and (12) give

    (z_1(N))_+<=kappa*sigma_1+(c-1)*C*sigma_2/2              (21)

provided gamma_1>0 and d*C*sigma_2<=gamma_1.
For the case above-threshold since the start, retain all prehistory.
With S_pre=S_(m-1)+b_(m-1), (6) gives

    z_1(0)<=beta+C*sigma_2*S_pre/2.

That branch cannot end above the threshold when
2beta+C*sigma_2*S_pre<=gamma_1*S.
All these finite conditions hold eventually by (13),
S_pre/S->lambda-1, and (lambda-2)*(lambda-1)=2-sqrt(2)<1.
Eventually the right side of (21) is <=2sigma_1. Therefore

    Phi_1(x(N))<=2sigma_1^2.                               (22)

For the last component write L_k=C*sigma_k. Its own checkpoint, at the
start of this suffix, gives r_k(0)=-beta*D_(k+1)<0.
Whenever r_k>L_k, Q_(k-1)=0 and P_k=L_k. An entry step adds at most
h*sigma_(k-1)/2. The last entry and its following H_j imply

    (r_k(N))_+<=L_k+(1/2) max_(0<=j<=m-2)
                                   [b_j*sigma_(k-1)-L_k*S_j]_+.

Set s=S_(m-2), T=b_(m-2)/2-1, Delta=d-C*T/M_k.
Since sigma_k/sigma_(k-1)=T/M_k exactly, (12) bounds this by

    L_k+(sigma_(k-1)/2)[c-1+(Delta)_+*s].

Pay the defect before taking asymptotics:

    d*M_k-C*T
       =d*(M_k-C*s/2)+C*(3-c)/2
                         +(C*d/2)(1-v_mass)*2^(m-2).

Equations(10),(11) give

    (Delta)_+*s <=(2d/C)K_k+3+d*(1-v_mass)*2^(m-2).
    A_err=c-1+(2d/C)K_k+3+d*(1-v_mass)*2^(m-2),
    Phi_k(x(N))<=L_k^2+(L_k*sigma_(k-1)/2)*A_err.            (23)

This is a finite bound, with the entire 2^(m-2) correction retained.
By (16) the benchmark-normalized sum of (22),(23) tends to zero.

These are jointly derived endpoint bounds in an UNVERIFIED report.
They are not accepted facts and cannot be used as accepted predecessors.

## 5. Schema A: exact terminal charge retaining later dissipation

For a differentiable convex E define its Bregman increment

    B_E(w^+,w)=E(w^+)-E(w)-<grad E(w),w^+-w>.

For E_B and g=Pi_B(w), g^+=Pi_B(w^+), expansion gives the equality

    B_E(w^+,w)
       =1/2||g^+-g||^2+<w^+-g^+,g^+-g>.                   (24)

The last pairing is nonnegative by the new projection's normal
inequality with the old g as a feasible comparison point.
Formula(24) also covers the last scalar Huber using B=[0,L_k].

For each actual suffix step use (24) at every centered pair, or at r_k.
Write B_i(t) for these increments. Summing first-order expansions gives

    V_m=2G_ret(x(0))-(1/2)sum_t h_t||d(t)||^2
                                           +sum_t sum_i B_i(t). (25)

This is equality for the actual coupled trajectory. In particular,
activity erased after a positive source is not discarded.

A second exact identity uses only ACTUAL TERMINAL projections.
Let g_i^N=(P_i(N),-Q_i(N)) for i<k and g_k^N=P_k(N).
Put N_N=sum_i P_i(N)^2+sum_(i<k)Q_i(N)^2. Since each terminal
gradient is fixed during the following algebra,

    V_m=sum_(i<k)[U_i(N)*r_i(0)+Q_i(N)*z_i(0)]
               +P_k(N)*r_k(0)-N_N/2
                      -(1/2)sum_t h_t<d(N),d(t)>.          (26)

Both neighbors enter the pairing. Explicitly,

    <d(N),d(t)>
       =sum_i P_i(N)P_i(t)+sum_(i<k)Q_i(N)Q_i(t)
        -sum_(i<k)[P_(i+1)(N)Q_i(t)+Q_i(N)P_(i+1)(t)].     (27)

If a component's terminal projection is zero it contributes no frozen
terminal charge. This is the useful distinction from all-time positive X.

Attempt A1 tried to dominate the Bregman terms in (25) by gradient
dissipation. The exact distance identity exhibits the obstruction.
For a fixed retired minimizer z_*=(A_1,ell_2,0,...,0), put

    N_grad=sum_i P_i^2+sum_(i<k)Q_i^2,
    C_cross=sum_(i<k)P_(i+1)Q_i,
    slack=-sum_(i<k)<(P_i,-Q_i),(z_*i-A_i,z_*(i+1)-ell_(i+1))>
                                      -P_k*(z_*k-A_k)>=0.

Expansion of (6) gives, with the ORIGINAL signs,

    ||x-z_*||^2-||x^+-z_*||^2
       =2h G_ret(x)+h(2-h)N_grad/4+h^2 C_cross/2+h*slack.   (28)

For h>2 the displayed gradient-square coefficient is negative.
No estimate in this screen pays it uniformly across all physical bridges.
For a scalar horizontal core r in (0,L_k), a large step sends
r^+=(1-h/2)r<0; its Bregman increment is (h/2-1/2)r^2.
Dropping these terms would destroy the exact cancellation that gives
terminal value zero in that elementary example.

Attempt A2 tried to make the cross-time pairing in (27) nonnegative.
That universal statement is false for the SAME frozen function.
Here is an explicit alternative-state test, not initialized reachability.

Choose an interior pair i,i+1, set x_i=A_i,
x_(i+1)=ell_(i+1)-kappa*sigma_i,
and x_(i+2)=ell_(i+2). Choose earlier coordinates sufficiently negative
that their components are zero at both samples, retaining the permanent
first-coordinate bound. Choose later coordinates zero, except for the
usual isolated terminal scalar. Initially g_i=(beta*sigma_i,-sigma_i),
g_(i+1)=0, and all other retired gradients vanish. A physical suffix
step h=b_(m-2) satisfying

    h>2kappa+2ell_(i+2)/(beta*sigma_i)+2C*sigma_(i+1)/sigma_i

sends component i into its zero cone and puts i+1 in its horizontal cap.
The next retired gradient is supported only at i+1 with value
C*sigma_(i+1). Thus

    <d_after,d_before>=-sigma_i*C*sigma_(i+1)<0.            (29)

The condition holds eventually because the threshold involving ell is
O(k exp(2k)), while b_(m-2) has exponential order lambda^m.

This refutes universal cross-time positivity. It does NOT refute a
trajectory-specific integrated inequality for (26).
The exact initialized pairing sum in (26), or an equally sharp signed
Bregman comparison in (25), is unpaid. No claim that actual X is small
or large is needed, and no actual terminal lower bound follows.

## 6. Schema B: own-checkpoint initialization and reachable retirement

This screen starts each interior i at its actual OWN checkpoint t_i;
it does not rename i as the first component. From (7),

    r_i(t_i)=-beta*D_(i+1),
    z_i(t_i)=-kappa*D_(i+1).                               (30)

For i<k the complete remaining physical word is

    (H_l_i,b_(l_i+1),H_(l_i+1),...,
                                  b_(m-1),H_(m-1)).        (31)

For i=k it is H_(m-1). The prefix ending at q_root includes all
intervening checkpoints and their ordinary blocks. Their parameters
are still the same enclosing m. Earlier coordinates in this prefix
follow (6); they are not placed back at their own checkpoint states.

In particular the incoming neighboring derivative at t_i is exactly

    Q_(i-1)(t_i)
      =-[Pi_(sigma_(i-1)K)(
          x_(i-1)(t_i)-A_(i-1),
                         ell_(i+1)/beta-beta*D_(i+1))]_2,
    x_(i-1)(t_i)=1_(i-1=1)
             -(1/2)sum_(u<t_i)h_u[P_(i-1)(u)-Q_(i-2)(u)]. (32)

This retains its full earlier physical history, including the incoming
derivative whenever it is nonzero. Nothing in (7) forces its vanishing;
no first-component isolation is imposed at retirement. The test below
exhibits a saturated nonzero value under the weaker checkpoint-margin
hypotheses, without asserting that alternative state is actually reached.
At the final-suffix start its value is the corresponding projection at
the actual coordinates given next. A clip formula for that projection
would require its OWN input bound; only the first permanent bound has
been proved in Section4.

For T>=t_i define exact finite integrals over (31)'s already executed
prefix,

    H_j^U(i,T)=(1/2)sum_(u=t_i)^(T-1)h_u U_j(u),
    H_j^Q(i,T)=(1/2)sum_(u=t_i)^(T-1)h_u Q_j(u).

BEFORE q_root the full Q_k is retained and U_k=P_k-beta*Q_k.
Put U_(k+1)=P_(k+1), Q_(k+1)=Q_0=0. Summing the full residual law,

    r_i(T)=-beta*D_(i+1)-H_i^U-beta*H_i^Q+H_(i-1)^Q,
    z_i(T)=-kappa*D_(i+1)-kappa*H_i^Q
              +beta*H_(i-1)^Q+beta*H_(i+1)^Q
                                   -beta*H_i^U+H_(i+1)^U. (33)

All H's on a line have the same (i,T). Thus (33) explicitly derives
the initialized incoming r_i(0),z_i(0) of the final suffix by T=q_root.
It also gives the ACTUAL terminal residuals by T=n. It retains both
neighbors and all later dissipation, rather than just its positive terms.

There is also a componentwise terminal-charge identity starting at this
OWN checkpoint, with no unknown replacement initial state. For i<k put
p_N=P_i(n), q_N=Q_i(n), u_N=p_N-beta*q_N, and use the FULL global
d_j(u)=P_j(u)-Q_(j-1)(u) even before q_root. Substitution of the
exact cumulative updates into the terminal maximizing formula gives

    Phi_i(x_n)=-beta*D_(i+1)*u_N-kappa*D_(i+1)*q_N
                -(p_N^2+q_N^2)/2
       -(1/2)sum_(u=t_i)^(n-1)h_u
                                  [p_N*d_i(u)-q_N*d_(i+1)(u)]. (33a)

The initial negative charge is explicit. The last bracket includes
p_N*P_i-p_N*Q_(i-1)-q_N*P_(i+1)+q_N*Q_i, with the ACTUAL
old and future gradients. It may have either sign; it is not replaced
by its positive terms. If p_N=q_N=0 the entire charge vanishes.
Paying these signed whole-future pairings from the initialized margins
is still open. The identity does not infer nonzero terminal value from
nonzero past activity.

An absorbing retired region DOES exist:

    C_ret={x: r_i<=0 for i<=k, z_i<=0 for i<k}.             (34)

If the actual suffix enters it, every retired projection is zero.
The only remaining scalar gradient acts on x_(k+1), and its excess
stays >=delta, so output isolation continues and (34) is preserved.
This proves conditional no-reactivation AFTER entrance, not entrance.

Attempt B1 sought entrance using the own-checkpoint margins (30).
Those margins, even supplemented by earlier negative checkpoint margins,
do not constitute an invariant for all states satisfying them.
The following exact test fixes that scope.

Take r_(i-1)=-beta*D_i, the own pair in (7), and still-future coordinates
zero. This is an ALTERNATIVE simultaneous state, not an assertion about
their later actual values. For i=2 it respects the permanent first bound.
Then

    z_(i-1)=beta*D_(i+1)-beta^2*D_i-ell_(i+1)/beta.

If this is >=kappa*sigma_(i-1), (18) gives the explicitly NONZERO
incoming Q_(i-1)=sigma_(i-1). These conditions hold eventually:
D_(i+1)/D_i is bounded away from0, beta=1/k, and
ell_(i+1)/beta and sigma_(i-1) are exponentially smaller than beta*D_i,
by (10)--(13). The own component has P_i=Q_i=0.
Its next component starts its next local block and has
P_(i+1)=C*sigma_(i+1), Q_(i+1)=0 eventually, since the large local
u_(i+1) satisfies the capped endpoint inequality.

Use the physical step h=b_(J+i-2), which occurs in the next complete
H_l_i. Since h*sigma_(i-1)/2=D_i+sigma_(i-1),

    r_i^+=-beta*D_(i+1)+D_i+sigma_(i-1)>0                 (35)

whenever beta*T_i/M_i<1. Also h*C<2M_(i+1) ensures its output
remains above ell_(i+1), so its new value is a positive horizontal Huber.
Both finite conditions hold eventually by (10)--(13).

Therefore the checkpoint-margin-only invariant fails under an available
physical step. The test does NOT refute retirement of the ACTUAL
initialized trajectory: the state at this later occurrence of h must be
proved reachable, and no such assertion is made. Its specific lesson is
that the actual incoming Q_(i-1), including its timing in (31), cannot
be discarded.

Attempt B2 used telescoping tail-coordinate weights. Define

    Z_i(x)=sum_(j=i)^(k+1) beta^(j-i)*x_j.

The full update, including Q_k before the root and the terminal scalar,
gives exactly

    Z_i^+=Z_i-(h/2)[sum_(j=i)^(k+1)beta^(j-i)U_j-Q_(i-1)].
    Z_i(t_i)=A_i+beta*ell_(i+1).                           (36)

The two D_(i+1) terms cancel in this INITIAL value, not by a reset.
For i=1 this weighted sum is nonincreasing. For every middle i it
retains the actual incoming source in (36), including all nonzero values.
Moreover a bound on Z_i alone does not bound positive r_i or z_i:
negative downstream coordinates can offset them. The cancelling adjacent
weight ratio is beta, whereas the scale ratios sigma_(i+1)/sigma_i
tend to lambda-2. A comparison between them loses factors of order
((lambda-2)/beta)^O(k) and does not pay the benchmark normalization.

The scoped unpaid initialized interface is now explicit: the signed
integrals in (33) up to q_root, their continuation through (31), and
entrance into (34). Neither attempt establishes their required inequalities.
The actual absorption conjecture is NOT refuted.

## 7. Schema C: pay circular cap loss by a downstream deficit

This is a genuinely different comparison from a beta*sigma_i cap floor.
Let 2<=i<=k-1, L_i=C*sigma_i, v_i=ell_(i+1)-x_(i+1), and set

    W_i=r_i+e*(v_i)_+, e=sqrt(1+2beta).                    (37)

Suppose an ACTUAL suffix step has r_i>L_i and r_i^+>L_i.
At its starting sample Q_(i-1)=0 by output isolation. Because r_i>L_i
exceeds every possible horizontal projection coordinate, the maximizer
lies on the upper circular boundary:

    P_i=sigma_i*p(q), Q_i=sigma_i*q, 0<=q<1.

Concavity and the endpoints give p(q)+e*q>=C, so

    P_i+e*Q_i>=L_i.                                       (38)

The output cannot cross from negative to nonnegative while this
horizontal excursion persists. To prove this rather than assume a
face invariant, take v_i>0. If q>0, the circular normal gives some t>=0,

    r_i=sigma_i*p(q)+t*(p(q)-beta),
    v_i=sigma_i*q+t*(q+beta),
    P_i*v_i-Q_i*r_i=sigma_i*t*beta*(p(q)+q)>=0.             (39)

For q=0 the same last inequality holds directly.
With alpha=h/2, r_i^+>L_i implies alpha*P_i<r_i-L_i<r_i.
Consequently alpha*Q_i<v_i when Q_i>0, and

    v_i^+=v_i+alpha*(P_(i+1)-Q_i)>0.

For Q_i=0 positivity also persists. This argument works for arbitrarily
large h and uses the actual updated horizontal inequality.

If v_i>0, expand (37) using this noncrossing result. If v_i<=0,
Q_i=0,P_i=L_i, and positive-part clipping can increase W_i by at most
alpha*e*P_(i+1). In both cases,

    W_i^+<=W_i-alpha*(L_i-e*P_(i+1))
            <=W_i-alpha*Gamma_i,
    Gamma_i=L_i-e*C*sigma_(i+1)
                         =C*(sigma_i-e*sigma_(i+1)).       (40)

If sigma_(i+1)/sigma_i<=1/2 and beta<=1/4, then e<=5/4 and

    Gamma_i>=3L_i/8>0.                                    (41)

These are explicit finite conditions, satisfied eventually by (13).
Both neighbors remain present: Q_(i-1) vanishes ONLY because the
actual excursion has x_i>A_i; P_(i+1) is paid with its full cap in (40).
No distance descent, h(2-h) deletion, independent pair evolution, or
circle cap floor is invoked.

In particular (40) pays the old tail cap loss. Writing
D_i^cap=(1/2)sum h*(L_i-P_i) during such an excursion, (38) gives
L_i-P_i<=e*Q_i. Instead of separately charging this positive integral,
(37)--(40) retain its downstream coordinate change and dissipation.
The following exact entry interface remains.

If r_i(N)>L_i, either the whole final suffix had r_i>L_i, in which case

    r_i(N)<=W_i(0)-Gamma_i*S/2,                            (42)

or there is a LAST entry step s with a=s+1, h_s=b_j, r_i(s)<=L_i
and r_i(a)>L_i. Necessarily Q_(i-1)(s)>0, hence x_i(s)<ell_i,
r_i(s)<0, U_i(s)=0 and P_i(s)=beta*Q_i(s).
The exact initialized entry cost is

    W_i(a)=r_i(s)+(b_j/2)(Q_(i-1)(s)-beta*Q_i(s))
       +e*[v_i(s)+(b_j/2)(P_(i+1)(s)-Q_i(s))]_+.           (43)

All later samples stay in r_i>L_i by the definition of LAST entry.
Therefore, retaining ALL subsequent dissipation,

    r_i(N)<=W_i(a)-Gamma_i*S_after(a)/2,                  (44)

where S_after(a) is the mass of every remaining physical step.
It includes a full H_j, so S_after(a)>=S_j. Equations(42)--(44) concern
the actual initialized trajectory; their entry data are fixed by
(32),(33), not freely selected.

Attempt C1 tried to pay the entry with only the global caps.
Dropping negative terms in (43), with r_i(s)<0, gives

    W_i(a)<=e*(v_i(s))_+
                     +(b_j/2)(sigma_(i-1)+e*C*sigma_(i+1)).

Even after paying the complete following H_j using (40), (12) leaves

    r_i(N)<=e*(v_i(s))_+
       +(c-1)(sigma_(i-1)+e*C*sigma_(i+1))/2
       +S_j*[d*sigma_(i-1)+e*(d+1)C*sigma_(i+1)-L_i]/2.   (45)

The last coefficient, divided by sigma_i, tends uniformly to

    d_0*(1+d_0)=2-sqrt(2)>0, d_0=lambda-2.

Indeed sigma_(i-1)/sigma_i->1/d_0,
sigma_(i+1)/sigma_i->d_0, and C,e->1.
For the possible last entry at the largest suffix bridge j=m-2,
the remaining word is precisely H_(m-2); no additional suffix mass
can be silently used. For i=2, multiplying this cap-based allowance
by L_2 and B_n/R_m^2 gives order lambda^(2k).
This is the divergent scale of a FAILED UPPER COMPARISON.
It is NOT a lower bound on actual terminal value and does not assert
that such a last entry is actually reached.

Thus the cap loss has been paid, but paying its entry by independent
neighbor caps throws away an essential initialized cancellation in (43).

Attempt C2 treated the no-entry branch (42) using full prehistory caps.
For i>=2, the actual update gives

    r_i(0)<=sigma_(i-1)*S_pre/2-A_i,
    (v_i(0))_+<=ell_(i+1)+C*sigma_(i+1)*S_pre/2.

The leading incoming allowance divided by Gamma_i*S/2 tends to

    [(1/d_0+d_0)*(lambda-1)]/(1-d_0)=4+2sqrt(2)>1.         (46)

The static threshold corrections are negligible uniformly, since
ell_(i+1)<=sigma_i*Y_k and S grows exponentially in m.
Therefore this particular comparison cannot exclude an always-positive
horizontal excursion. As with (45), (46) is not a claim about the actual
incoming vector. The exact vector is (33).

Counterexample screening does not refute the persistence estimate (40):
the projection normal proves its needed no-crossing condition.
It does refute extending it outside its hypotheses. During an entry,
Q_(i-1) is explicitly nonzero, and when r_i<=L_i, the same upper-arc
floor (38) need not hold. The checkpoint-margin test (35) is an explicit
same-function example of such an entry. Using (40) there without (43)
would lose the initializing source.

Finally (40) controls a terminal positive HORIZONTAL excursion. A full
middle-value bound also needs its actual edge residual z_i(N), including
when r_i(N)<=L_i. The exact residuals (33) and terminal identity (26)
retain that edge contribution. No all-time positive-X estimate or
independent edge evolution has been substituted for it.

## 8. Exact quantitative handoff and remaining theorem-level gaps

Define the ACTUAL middle terminal value

    V_mid,m=sum_(i=2)^(k-1)Phi_i(x(N)).

Set the explicit endpoint loss

    epsilon_end,m=(B_n/R_m^2)*
          [2sigma_1^2+L_k^2+(L_k*sigma_(k-1)/2)*A_err].

Under the finite conditions stated in Section4, its jointly derived
bounds give epsilon_end,m->0 and

    T_m+(B_n/R_m^2)V_mid,m
      <=B_n*2F(x_n)/R_m^2
      <=T_m+(B_n/R_m^2)V_mid,m+epsilon_end,m.              (47)

For every middle component, (17) gives the unconditional finite bound

    Phi_i(x(N))<=L_i*(r_i(N))_+ +sigma_i*(z_i(N))_+.        (48)

For those with r_i(N)>L_i, define their actual paid-entry remainder as
the right side of (42), or of (44), according to their actual last entry.
For r_i(N)<=L_i use L_i. Equations(42)--(44),(48) give a finite
upper comparison retaining the later dissipation, but no estimate
for its initialized remainders or terminal edge terms is established.

A uniform residual control at the scale
sigma_1^2*(1+K_k+2^m), with at most polynomial factors in k, would be
negligible at the original benchmark by (16). This describes a possible
loss scale, not a proved hypothesis and not a new unrestricted program.

The concrete unpaid interfaces are:

1. In schema A, the ACTUAL integrated cross-time pairing in (26),(27),
   or the signed dissipation/Bregman balance in (25). Universal
   cross-time positivity is false by (29); the initialized integrated
   comparison is neither proved nor refuted.

2. In schema B, the full ordered-prefix integrals in (33), with the
   actual incoming Q_(i-1) in (32), and reachable entrance into (34).
   Own-checkpoint margins alone fail the available-step test (35).
   No actual-trajectory absorption theorem follows from that test.

3. In schema C, the initialized W_i(0) or the exact entry W_i(a) in
   (43), together with the terminal edge residual. Its cap loss IS
   paid by (40); the entry cap-only replacement (45) and incoming
   cap-only replacement (46) fail at the required scale.

There is no uniform bound on B_n*V_mid,m/R_m^2 below 1-T_infty,
no proof of its vanishing, and no proof of exact absorption.
There is also no actual terminal lower witness.

The high-precision m16 display from2df3df199e391be9 only motivated retaining
cancellation: displayed zero middle values coexist with positive scaled X.
No displayed zero, growing-k trend, m>=1536 claim, or exact numerical
value is used in this proof attempt. Binary64 m16/m20 are not reliable
evidence about the exact trajectory, as bfed68e4c77df24e records.

A successful strict terminal upper for this ONE frozen function would
obstruct this policy only. It would not upper-bound unrestricted W_n
and would not resolve the original minimax question. A successful lower
would still concern only H_(m,1/4) at physical horizons2^m-1. The original
all-word/all-horizon target remains OPEN.

## 9. Retrieval, verification, resources and closure

Skills used: query-memory, direct-proving, construct-counterexamples,
search-math-results. Search stage mature_subproblem; mode repair;
missing mechanism initialized terminal dissipation/retirement in this
specific coupled projection chain. No new mathematical program was opened.

Fact searches found no accepted initialized circular-middle or cap-loss
result. The relevant statement and proof of fact04219554e41d9750 were read;
its benchmark/ray scope alone is used. Other accepted spiral families
are different constructions and are not premises.

Matlas searches for exact long-step identification and coupled circle
cap cancellation returned results for unrelated nonsmooth algorithms,
neural-network gradient flows/perturbations, and stochastic bandwidth
sharing. None supplies the fixed-step initialized interface above.
Web fallback returned existing Silver/composition background and
different projected or line-search identification settings. No external
theorem is imported or used as a black box, and no new literature branch
requiring a new exact-paper layer was opened.

The proof attempts and their counterexample scopes remain joint unverified.
The usual fact_submit promotion gate was explicitly forbidden by TASK.md
and current guidance. It was not called. No fact graph file was edited.

Mathematical programs0; numerical evaluations0; arithmetic replays0;
trajectory runs0; optimizer/solver/DP0; formal calls0; new accepted facts0;
installations0; consults0; subagents0. Only shared/public text reading,
theorem searches, symbolic textual reasoning, administrative text writes
and file-integrity metadata operations were performed.

Exactly three analytical schemas were screened. The one allocated round
is finished with the explicit scoped failures above. Expected ended count
after return63; the official post-return counter has not been observed.
No automatic renewal, finalization, revocation, publication, project
shutdown or original Goal completion is performed. No work is in flight.

# R4 handoff: internal auxiliaries see the old defects, but local long-bridge payment fails

JOINT UNVERIFIED. Guidance `d22feaa7fe6d4c3f`.
One analytical invocation, two laws, mathematical programs/formal calls
zero. STOP; parent closes the ledger. Original target OPEN.

## New actual geometry

For every original physical boundary set

    D_i=1+2 sum_(t=i)^(n-1) h_t,
    q_i=P_D([x0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t]/D_i).

These are actual internal block entrance/exit resolvents in the same
fixed D. Identify q_n=g_n and every shared block-boundary label. A block
[ell,r) has entrance scale D_ell=D_r+2S_block and exit scale D_r.
The physical trajectory is not reset or altered.

On the old cancelling support, x20=x23=x24=-(C2+k2)v. Both laws now
include internal resolvents at nonzero inputs, so the old all-zero-input
obstruction is not being reused.

## The two complete laws

1. **Every physical step:** telescope the full one-step primitive,
   radial and maximization identities at the scales D_i. REPORT (4.3)
   gives every original nonnegative multiplier. The full residual is

       eta D0||q0||²
       +sum_i [(2-h_i²)/D_i ||g_i-g_(i+1)||²
               +D_i||q_i-[h_i g_i+(D_(i+1)+h_i)q_(i+1)]/D_i||²]
       +sum_(D_(i+1)>1) Gamma(h_i,D_(i+1))/D_i
                           ||g_(i+1)-q_(i+1)||²,

   where eta=1/16 and
   Gamma(s,d)=[2sd+s²(d+1)]/(d-1). At d=1 the corresponding radial
   term and rows are omitted because its actual gradients coincide.
2. **Accepted H3 transfer units:** partition the actual H_m into its
   H3 blocks and intervening original large bridges. Each H3 uses the
   COMPLETE accepted H3 certificate at its actual trailing scale,
   including both H2 arrays, both maximization squares, bridge reserve
   and the exact radial square/rows. Each intervening bridge uses Law
   1's one-step identity. REPORT (5.3)--(5.8) specifies every original
   coefficient and the entire global residual.

Both laws use root star coefficient `2 eta D0`; all original coefficients
are nonnegative. Neither drops a signed primitive or maximization term.

## New exact full-space obstruction

Take the top physical bridge i=2^(m-1)-1, with b=h_i and D=D_i. Assign

    g_i=v != 0, q_i=b v/(2D),

and every other physical and auxiliary gradient zero. Set x0=0 and
retain the original affine physical positions. All duplicate-label
identifications are respected, and the root allowance is zero.

For Law 1 only the preceding step A=1+a and the bridge contribute. The
FULL residual has scalar coefficient E1, REPORT (4.5), and

    E1 < [10+1/8-b²/2]/D < 0 for b>=5.

For Law 2 only the preceding complete H3 and the bridge contribute.
The entire H3 contribution is

    [e2+Gamma(S2,D)(1-b/(2D))²]/(D+2S2)
      +(D+S2)²/(D+2S2) * b²/(4D²).

Together with the full bridge term, this gives

    E2 < [70+25/16-b²/2]/D < 0 for b>=16.

The analytical bounds used are S2<5, e2<31, Gamma(S2,D)<37 for D>=33,
and D>2b. No mathematical program was run. The actual top bridge has
b=1+beta c^(m-2)>16 for every m>=6, hence for every requested m>=1536.

This direction is NOT a legal GD trajectory: x_i=0 but q_i is assigned
nonzero. It is a full-matrix PSD witness only. The two laws fail; the
desired all-body U inequality and original minimax target remain open.

## Scope and remaining gap

The exclusions concern exactly the displayed local transfer rules and
their fixed H3 strengthening. They do not exclude a growing original-row
coupling across a large bridge, different internal scales, another star
allocation, or a general internal-resolvent certificate. The H3 terminal
reserve is bounded while the long-bridge negative term grows as b²/D.
A general tree energy theorem has not been matched to this coefficient
graph and is not used as payment.

The full polar minimum set and original W=2R implication are stated in
REPORT Section 7. An actual eventual U upper bound would still imply
`liminf W_n^*/U_n^ZJ<=799/800` through the two accepted facts, but its
hypothesis remains unproved.

## Accounting

TASK hash matched
`e86592bc1a2383840c726716ebe9cc7e896e09465e7ad54f75525ff1ccb2aae9`.
Only the new R4 output directory was written. No numerical files,
mathematical scripts, array reads, formal calls, extra agents/models,
worker/service actions or truth-store changes. Parent owns ledger
closeout. All new mathematics JOINT UNVERIFIED. STOP, no renewal.

During closeout the parent relayed new operator steering: subsequent work
should focus on direct all-depth closure from Zhang--Jiang's
Silver/primitive-dominant proof, not further equivalent formulations or
fixed-size models. No third law or additional call was opened here.

# Internal entrance/exit resolvents: two complete transfer laws and a long-bridge obstruction

JOINT UNVERIFIED. Fresh guidance `d22feaa7fe6d4c3f`, 2026-10-08.
One analytical invocation; original minimax target OPEN.

## 1. Outcome

Two explicit laws were constructed on the actual internal-boundary
geometry `q_i=P_D(x_i/D_i)`, with D_i equal to one plus twice the entire
remaining physical step mass. These inputs include the entrances and exits
of internal H2/H3 blocks. They see the two cancelling defects that were
invisible to the previous initial-only/right-suffix-only construction.

Law 1 telescopes the complete one-step U identity over every original
physical step. Law 2 retains the same actual geometry but replaces every
H3 portion by the complete accepted H3 identity, including both H2
residuals, radial rows, bridge rows and maximization remainders. Large
bridges between those H3 blocks remain individual physical steps.

Every original multiplier and the entire residual are specified below.
All multipliers are nonnegative. Both full residual matrices nevertheless
have an exact negative direction at a sufficiently long bridge. For a
bridge step b at physical index i, let D=D_i. The direction has only

\[
 g_i=v,\qquad q_i=\frac{b}{2D}v,\qquad v\ne0,
\tag{1.1}
\]

nonzero among the physical and auxiliary gradients. It respects all
fixed duplicate-label identifications. The complete residuals satisfy

\[
 \mathcal R^{(1)}<\frac{10+1/8-b^2/2}{D}\|v\|^2<0
 \quad(b\ge5),
\tag{1.2}
\]

\[
 \mathcal R^{(2)}<\frac{70+25/16-b^2/2}{D}\|v\|^2<0
 \quad(b\ge16).
\tag{1.3}
\]

The actual top bridge of every requested H_m has b>16. Thus both laws
are excluded on the full actual hierarchy for every m>=1536, without
computing any trajectory or matrix spectrum.

This is a new local obstruction involving an internal auxiliary with a
nonzero formal value. It is not the old zero-aggregate kernel argument.
The direction is not a legal GD trajectory and does not refute the desired
U inequality. No all-depth upper certificate was obtained; these two laws
are BLOCKED.

## 2. Sources, fixed hierarchy, and actual auxiliary geometry

The current OPERATOR, original PROBLEM and complete fresh TASK were read.
The previous multi-resolvent report was retained under its checked digest;
its HANDOFF and MAIN_READBACK were read, including the clarification that
the primitive join's fixed endpoint rows occur once per join. That older
signed-join recursion is not used by either new law.

The designated accepted H3 proof, Part B Sections 4--6, was reread with
all displayed coefficients and original rows. Its H2 table supplies the
finite base used below. The parent's radial-reserve note was also read;
the exact identity is rederived in Section 3. The old initial-chain and
two-feature gluing laws are not retuned. No numerical files were opened.

For one fixed enclosing m>=1536, keep

\[
 \varepsilon=1/(4m),\quad \beta=1-\varepsilon,\quad
 c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\varepsilon),\quad a=\beta/c
\]

in every child of `H_0=()` and `H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+ac^j,H_j)`.
Write the full physical word as `h_0,...,h_(n-1)`, n=2^m-1, and set

\[
 D_i=1+2\sum_{t=i}^{n-1}h_t,\qquad
 q_i=P_D(x_i/D_i),\qquad 0\le i\le n.
\tag{2.1}
\]

In particular `D_0=M_m`, `D_n=1`, and `q_n=g_n` is one identified
label. For every i<n, D_i>1 and q_i is generally distinct from g_i.
Every input is the fixed affine expression

\[
 X_{q_i}=\frac{x_0-\sum_{t<i}h_tg_t}{D_i}.
\tag{2.2}
\]

For any actual dyadic block B=[ell,r) of mass S_B, its entrance and exit
scales are exactly `D_ell=D_r+2S_B` and `D_r`. Thus (2.1) is the proposed
internal-block geometry, implemented on the original time boundaries.
Shared block-boundary labels are identified. No physical start is reset,
no auxiliary gradient is selected independently, and one fixed compact
convex D containing zero is used throughout.

Original normal rows are

\[
 Q_{uv}=\langle X_v-G_v,G_u-G_v\rangle\le0,
\tag{2.3}
\]

with physical inputs X_i=x_i and gradients G_i=g_i, auxiliary inputs
(2.2) and gradients q_i, and the zero star. The desired sufficient
certificate has nonnegative original coefficients and globally PSD

\[
 \alpha U_{D_0}(x_0)-U_1(x_n)+\sum\lambda_{uv}Q_{uv},
 \qquad \alpha=17/16,quad\eta=\alpha-1=1/16.
\tag{2.4}
\]

Throughout, actual U values are replaced by their expressions at the
specified actual maximizing gradients before the formal quadratic is
formed. No common-gradient contact, absorption, or terminal-zero quotient
is imposed.

## 3. Local radial and maximization identities, with all original rows

For an actual physical block of mass s, entrance x, exit z and trailing
scale d>=1, write

\[
 M=d+2s,\quad g=P_D(z),\quad q=P_D(z/d),\quad p=P_D(x/M),
\]

\[
 \bar z=\frac{x-z+(d+s)q}{M},\qquad
 V=\|x\|^2-\|z+s g\|^2.
\]

The accepted complete identity is

\[
 U_M(x)-U_d(z)=\frac{V+L_{\rm rad}(s,z,d)}{M}
   +M\|p-\bar z\|^2
   -2\sum_{t\in B}h_tQ_{t,p}-2(d+s)Q_{q,p}.
\tag{3.1}
\]

It follows by evaluating U_M at the convex mixture bar z and retaining
the exact maximization remainder. The physical weights h_t and auxiliary
weight d+s sum to M.

For d>1 set

\[
 \Gamma(s,d)=\frac{2sd+s^2(d+1)}{d-1},\quad
 L_1(s,d)=\frac{2s(d+s)}{d-1},\quad
 L_2(s,d)=\frac{2sd(1+s)}{d-1}.
\tag{3.2}
\]

The radial term has the exact original-row decomposition

\[
 \boxed{L_{\rm rad}(s,z,d)
 =\Gamma(s,d)\|g-q\|^2-L_1(s,d)Q_{q,g}-L_2(s,d)Q_{g,q}.}
\tag{3.3}
\]

To check it independently, put e=g-q,
`N1=<z-g,e>=-Q_(q,g)` and `N2=<z-dq,-e>=-d Q_(g,q)`.
Their sum is `(d-1)<q,e>-||e||^2`. Substitute this equality and
`<z,e>=<q,e>+||e||^2+N1` into the radial term. The square coefficient is
Gamma, the N1 coefficient is L1, and the N2 coefficient is L2/d. This
gives (3.3) with no inequality or omitted cross term.

At d=1, q=g is identified and Lrad=0. The singular expressions (3.2)
are not used. In residual formulas below the radial summand is omitted
at that endpoint, rather than interpreted by a limit.

## 4. Law 1: fully refined transfer at every physical step

For step i put `h=h_i`, `d=D_(i+1)`, `M=D_i=d+2h`. The exact signed
one-step primitive identity is

\[
 V_{(h)}+2Q_{i,i+1}+2(1+h)Q_{i+1,i}
   =(2-h^2)\|g_i-g_{i+1}\|^2.
\tag{4.1}
\]

It is obtained by expanding the two original physical rows and the
actual update. It is valid for every h>=0; its square coefficient is
retained with its actual sign.

Define

\[
 z_i=\frac{h_i g_i+(D_{i+1}+h_i)q_{i+1}}{D_i}.
\tag{4.2}
\]

The complete original multiplier list for each step is

\[
 \begin{array}{ll}
 \lambda_{i,i+1}=2/D_i,&
 \lambda_{i+1,i}=2(1+h_i)/D_i,\\
 \lambda_{q_{i+1},i+1}=L_1(h_i,D_{i+1})/D_i,&
 \lambda_{i+1,q_{i+1}}=L_2(h_i,D_{i+1})/D_i,\\
 \lambda_{i,q_i}=2h_i,&
 \lambda_{q_{i+1},q_i}=2(D_{i+1}+h_i).
 \end{array}
\tag{4.3}
\]

The radial second line is included only when D_(i+1)>1. All coefficients
are nonnegative. Repeated ordered rows are added, and identified
diagonal rows are omitted. Every unlisted row is zero.

Summing (3.1) over the entire actual word telescopes its U boundary
values, because each q_i and scale D_i is shared. Add the original star
row `lambda_(*,q0)=2 eta D_0`. Its exact contribution to the relaxed
allowance is

\[
 \eta U_{D_0}(x_0)+2\eta D_0Q_{*,q_0}
 =\eta D_0\|q_0\|^2.
\]

The full Law 1 residual is therefore

\[
 \boxed{\begin{split}
 \mathcal R^{(1)}={}&\eta D_0\|q_0\|^2\\
 &+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\left[
 \frac{2-h_i^2}{D_i}\|g_i-g_{i+1}\|^2
 +D_i\|q_i-z_i\|^2\right]\\
 &+\sum_{\{i:D_{i+1}>1\}}
 \frac{\Gamma(h_i,D_{i+1})}{D_i}\|g_{i+1}-q_{i+1}\|^2.
 \end{split}}
\tag{4.4}
\]

This is the entire quadratic matrix as an explicit sum of squared linear
forms, some with negative coefficients. No primitive, radial or
maximization term has been discarded. In particular the new internal
auxiliaries are coupled to physical gradients at every original step.

### Exact long-bridge obstruction to Law 1

Let i be an internal physical bridge with step b>=5 and preceding step
`A=1+a`, where `1<A<sqrt(2)`. This applies at the top join of the actual
H_m. Put D=D_i, so `D_(i-1)=D+2A` and D>2b. Use the full formal
assignment (1.1), with every other physical and auxiliary gradient zero,
and x0=0. The original affine physical recursion is retained. The global
root auxiliary q0 and terminal labels q_n=g_n are zero.

Only steps i-1 and i contribute to (4.4). Write `t=b/(2D)`, so 0<t<1/4.
Substituting literally into every term of (4.4) gives the full scalar
coefficient

\[
 \begin{split}
 E_1={}&\frac{2-b^2}{D}+D(t-b/D)^2
       +\frac{2-A^2}{D+2A}\\
     &+\frac{\Gamma(A,D)}{D+2A}(1-t)^2
       +\frac{(D+A)^2}{D+2A}t^2.
 \end{split}
\tag{4.5}
\]

There are no omitted contributions from the bridge's exit radial term:
both its exit physical gradient and exit auxiliary are zero. The previous
radial and maximization terms are fully present in (4.5).

Since D>=2b+1>=11, A<3/2 and A^2<2 give

\[
 \Gamma(A,D)=A^2+2A+\frac{2A^2+2A}{D-1}<6,
 \qquad
 \frac{(D+A)^2}{D+2A}=D+\frac{A^2}{D+2A}<D+2/D.
\]

Using also `0<2-A^2<2`, substitution of t in (4.5) yields

\[
 E_1<\frac{10-b^2/2+b^2/(2D^2)}{D}
     <\frac{10+1/8-b^2/2}{D}<0.
\tag{4.6}
\]

The last strict sign follows already from b>=5. This rules out the full
matrix of Law 1, despite the complete internal radial and maximization
couplings.

## 5. Law 2: retain the complete accepted H3 blocks

The second and final law changes the physical transfer units. Recursively
cut H_m at depth three. This partitions the original word into actual
H3 blocks and the original bridges of levels at least three, in the
unchanged order. A H3 block starts at a multiple of eight; each large
bridge lies between two such blocks. All remaining steps are represented
exactly once.

Each large bridge uses (4.1)--(4.3) at its actual global trailing scale.
Each H3 block instead uses the complete accepted H3 certificate described
next. Its boundary auxiliaries remain the shared q_i from (2.1).

Set

\[
 A=1+a,\quad B=1+\beta,\quad s=S_2=2A+B,
 \quad C=1+s,\quad k=k_2=\beta c,\quad \omega=C+k.
\]

For a H3 block [ell,e), e=ell+7, put

\[
 d=D_e,\quad T=d+2s=D_{\ell+4},\quad
 M=T+2\omega=D_\ell,\quad b_*=\ell+3,
\]

\[
 p=q_\ell,\quad r=q_{\ell+4},\quad q=q_e,\quad g=g_e,
 \qquad
 \Theta=\frac{\omega[CT-k(C+T+k)]}{T+k}.
\tag{5.1}
\]

Thus the interior resolvent r has its actual input `x_(ell+4)/T`.
For d=1, q=g is identified. The two complete maximization mixtures are

\[
 z_P=\frac{\sum_{i=\ell}^{\ell+3}h_i g_i+(T+\omega)r}{M},
 \qquad
 z_R=\frac{\sum_{i=\ell+4}^{\ell+6}h_i g_i+(d+s)q}{T}.
\tag{5.2}
\]

For explicit physical coefficients, let C1=1+A and define

\[
 K_1=\frac{3C1+\beta}{C1-\beta},\qquad
 R_1=\frac{(C1+\beta)[C1^2-\beta(2C1+\beta)]}{C1-\beta}>0.
\]

The H2 original-row table, in local indices 0,1,2,3, is

\[
 \begin{array}{ll}
 \Lambda_{01}=2,&\Lambda_{10}=2(1+A),\\
 \Lambda_{12}=2R_1/B,&\Lambda_{21}=(K_1-1)A+2R_1/B,\\
 \Lambda_{13}=(K_1-1)\beta,&\Lambda_{31}=K_1-1,\\
 \Lambda_{23}=2K_1+(K_1-1)A+2R_1/A,&
 \Lambda_{32}=2K_1(1+A)+2R_1/A.
 \end{array}
\tag{5.3}
\]

All other entries are zero. Each entry is added once for that H2 block,
with the indicated local-to-global index shift. Its entire physical
residual is

\[
 F_2=e_0\|g_0-g_1\|^2+e_1\|g_1-g_2\|^2
       +e_2\|g_2-g_3\|^2,
\]

\[
 e_0=2-A^2,\quad e_1=R_1(2/B-1),\quad
 e_2=K_1(2-A^2)+R_1(2/A-1)>0.
\tag{5.4}
\]

The accepted H2 proof gives all these positive signs under the same m.
They can also be checked from A<sqrt(2), B<2 and
`(sqrt(2)-1)C1-beta>0`, which factors the numerator defining R1.

For this H3 block, every additional original coefficient is as follows:

1. Add the left H2 table (5.3) with scale 1/M and the right H2 table
   with scale 1/T.
2. Add the bridge rows

\[
 \lambda_{r,b_*}=\frac{2\omega}{M}\left(1+\frac C{T+k}\right),
 \qquad
 \lambda_{b_*,r}=\frac{2\omega CT}{M(T+k)}.
\tag{5.5}
\]

3. Add the two maximization-row families

\[
 \begin{array}{ll}
 \lambda_{i,p}=2h_i&(\ell\le i\le\ell+3),\\
 \lambda_{r,p}=2(T+\omega),&\\
 \lambda_{i,r}=2h_i&(\ell+4\le i\le\ell+6),\\
 \lambda_{q,r}=2(d+s).&
 \end{array}
\tag{5.6}
\]

4. If d>1, add `lambda_(q,g)=L1(s,d)/T` and
   `lambda_(g,q)=L2(s,d)/T`. At d=1 omit those identified zero rows.

All entries in (5.3), (5.5)--(5.6) and the radial rows are nonnegative.
Duplicated ordered rows from any source are added, with identical-input
labels already identified. There are no unspecified coefficients.

The full H3 residual supplied by this table is

\[
 \boxed{\mathcal H_3(d)={}
 \frac{F_2^{L}}M+\frac{F_2^{R}}T
 +\frac\Theta M\|g_{b_*}-r\|^2
 +M\|p-z_P\|^2+T\|r-z_R\|^2
 +\frac{\Gamma(s,d)}T\|g-q\|^2.}
\tag{5.7}
\]

The last summand is omitted at d=1. All terms are retained. The accepted
H3 proof ensures Theta>0 for every d>=1: C>5, k<C/2 and T>=2C-1 give
`CT-k(C+T+k)>C(C-2)/4>0`. Thus each H3 unit itself has a fully positive
certificate, including all original cross terms inside its squares.

Summing these H3 identities and the large-bridge identities telescopes
the U values at their common actual boundary inputs. Add the same root
star coefficient `2 eta D_0`. The complete second-law residual is

\[
 \boxed{\mathcal R^{(2)}=
 \eta D_0\|q_0\|^2+
 \sum_{\text{actual H3 blocks}}\mathcal H_3(D_{\rm exit})
 +\sum_{\text{large bridges}}\mathcal L_i,}
\tag{5.8}
\]

where `L_i` is precisely the three-summand local residual in (4.4),
including its signed primitive square, complete radial square and
complete maximization square. Formula (5.8), together with the full
coefficient tables, is the entire global quadratic. No primitive
positivity is assumed for H_j with j>=3.

### Exact long-bridge obstruction to Law 2

Let i be the top physical bridge of H_m, let b=h_i>=16 and D=D_i, and
take the same full formal assignment (1.1), with all other coordinates
zero. The seven physical steps immediately before i form one actual H3.
Its nonterminal physical gradients are zero and its terminal gradient
is g_i=v. Its endpoint auxiliary is q_i=t v, t=b/(2D), while its entrance
auxiliary and internal r are zero.

In that preceding H3 certificate, the left H2 residual is zero. The
right H2 residual is exactly e2||v||^2. The bridge square and prefix
maximization square are zero. The entire remaining contribution is

\[
 \frac{e_2}{D+2s}\|v\|^2
 +\frac{\Gamma(s,D)}{D+2s}(1-t)^2\|v\|^2
 +\frac{(D+s)^2}{D+2s}t^2\|v\|^2.
\tag{5.9}
\]

Every other H3 unit vanishes. At the large bridge, the exit physical and
auxiliary gradients are zero, so its full contribution is the first two
terms below. No other large bridge contributes. Thus the entire scalar
coefficient is exactly

\[
 E_2=\frac{2-b^2}{D}+D(t-b/D)^2
    +\frac{e_2+\Gamma(s,D)(1-t)^2}{D+2s}
    +\frac{(D+s)^2}{D+2s}t^2.
\tag{5.10}
\]

Here is a uniform analytical bound on all positive terms. We have s<5.
Also `2<C1<5/2`, `0<beta<1` give

\[
 K_1<17/2,\qquad R_1<175/8,\qquad e_2<243/8<31,
\]

using `0<2-A^2<1` and `0<2/A-1<1`. Since D>=2b+1>=33,

\[
 \Gamma(s,D)=s^2+2s+\frac{2s^2+2s}{D-1}<37,
 \quad
 \frac{(D+s)^2}{D+2s}=D+\frac{s^2}{D+2s}<D+25/D.
\]

Substituting t into (5.10) now gives

\[
 E_2<\frac{70-b^2/2+25b^2/(4D^2)}D
     <\frac{70+25/16-b^2/2}D<0.
\tag{5.11}
\]

The complete accepted H3 reserve therefore does not repair this law's
long-bridge direction. This conclusion retains both H2 residuals and
every radial and maximization term; it is not an estimate based only on
an adjacent-gradient subreserve.

## 6. Applicability to the full hierarchy and relation to the old screen

The top bridge index is `i=2^(m-1)-1`. Its preceding physical step is
the last step A=1+a of H_(m-1). For m>=4 its preceding seven physical
steps form the terminal H3 of that child. Its bridge size is

\[
 b=1+ac^{m-1}=1+\beta c^{m-2}.
\]

For m>=6, `beta>99/100` and c>2 imply b>1+16(99/100)>16.
Also `D_i=1+2[b+S_(m-1)]>2b+1`. Thus every hypothesis of both
negative estimates holds for every requested enclosing m>=1536.

The directions are tested in each full original-coordinate quadratic.
Set x0=0 and construct every physical x_j from its original affine
recursion. Set all coordinates other than (1.1) to zero. In particular,
q_n=g_n=0 and every shared block-boundary label receives one value.
The relaxed allowance term is zero because q0=0.

This is not an actual projection history: the input x_i/D_i at the
active auxiliary is zero whereas its assigned q_i is nonzero. It also
has g0=0 followed by a later nonzero physical gradient. These facts make
it an inadmissible GD trajectory, but do not invalidate it as a test of
global PSD. No actual objective-error lower bound or counterexample is
claimed.

The geometry genuinely changes the old zero-aggregate screen. On the old
formal support `g16..g19=v`, `g24..g27=-v`, its physical internal points
satisfy

\[
 x_{20}=x_{23}=x_{24}=-(C_2+k_2)v,\qquad x_{28}=0
 \quad(x_0=0).
\]

Both new laws include actual resolvents at some of these nonzero inputs:
Law 1 includes every boundary, while Law 2 includes the first H3's
internal q20 and the next H3's entrance q24. Consequently the premise
that all new auxiliary inputs vanish no longer holds.

There is also an explicit positive term if the old screen is extended
by setting the new auxiliary coordinates formally to zero: each active
H3 in Law 2 has bridge square and prefix maximization square totaling
`[Theta+omega^2]||v||^2/M>0`. The new failure (5.11) was therefore
checked on a different direction, not inferred from the previous
exclusion.

The exact scope is the two displayed coefficient laws, with their
specified remaining-mass scales, local transfer formulas and root
allowance row. They do not exclude a growing coupling across both sides
of each large bridge, different internal scales, redistribution of
original physical--auxiliary multipliers, or another use of the relaxed
star budget. In particular, the fixed H3 transfer unit provides bounded
terminal coefficients while b grows; the proof excludes that specific
payment rule, not all internal-resolvent tree certificates.

## 7. Original target and the remaining mathematical gap

For the complete polar minimum set

\[
 C_D=\{z:\langle z,g\rangle\le0\text{ for every }g\in D\},
\]

the projection function has `argmin F_D=C_D` and minimum zero. Every
selected minimum z satisfies

\[
 U_{M_m}(x_0)\le\operatorname{dist}(x_0,C_D)^2/M_m
              \le\|x_0-z\|^2/M_m.
\]

Hence a genuine all-body/all-start bound
`U1(T_Hm x)<=17 U_Mm(x)/16` would give the full-class
`W(H_m)<=17/(16M_m)` by accepted finite-word projection-simplex
universality, separately for each fixed word. Together with accepted
`B_(2^m-1)/M_m<47/50`, this would give
`liminf W_n^*/U_n^ZJ<=799/800<1`. The factor-two normalization W=2R
and every selected minimizer are retained; no radius is replaced by
distance to the origin.

That sufficient U inequality remains unproved. A successful replacement
would need explicit original coefficients that control the long-bridge
mixed direction while preserving all other terms and the 17/16 budget.
A generic tree-Carleson, Hardy, or Schur statement without the matched
coefficient graph would not establish this payment. No such unmatched
theorem is used here.

## 8. Accounting and closeout

Exactly one native analytical invocation and exactly two explicit laws
were used. Mathematical programs, calculator calls, recurrence/symbolic
replays, numerical arrays, diagnostics, solvers, scans, formal calls,
extra agents/models, consults, installations, worker/service actions,
truth-store writes, publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown and
Goal mutations were all zero. Only text reads, manual derivation,
Markdown writing and byte hashing were used.

`e86592bc1a2383840c726716ebe9cc7e896e09465e7ad54f75525ff1ccb2aae9`.
The prior report hash matched
`e3570d96fdcf058207962186d2be0084a5ac35c82d19161340fce21484507c90`.
Only the new R4 output directory was written. The parent owns analytical
ledger registration and closeout; no old allocation was renewed.

During closeout the parent relayed the operator's new direction: subsequent
work should seek direct all-depth closure from the Zhang--Jiang

or fixed-size models. This invocation only finishes its two already
